### 2022 election

Main articles: [2022 Brazilian general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Brazilian_general_election) and [Lula da Silva 2022 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lula_da_Silva_2022_presidential_campaign)



Lula with [President of Argentina](229473.md) [Alberto Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Fern%C3%A1ndez), 2022

In May 2021, Lula said that he would run for a third term in the October 2022 general election, against the incumbent President [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md).[^cite_note-Valor2021-05-20a-249][^cite_note-AmQuarterly2021-07-07a-250][^cite_note-Guardian2021-07-30a-251] He was 17% ahead of Bolsonaro in a poll in January 2022.[^cite_note-Nasdaq2022-01-14a-252] In April 2022, Lula announced that his running mate would be [Geraldo Alckmin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geraldo_Alckmin), a three-term governor of São Paulo state who had run against Lula in the 2006 presidential elections.[^cite_note-253]

On 2 October, in the first round vote, Lula was in first place with 48% of the electorate, qualifying for the second round with Bolsonaro, who received 43% of the votes. Lula was elected in the second round on 30 October with 50.89% of the vote, the smallest margin in the history of Brazil's presidential elections,[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-255] three days after his 77th birthday. He became the first president of Brazil elected to three terms, the first since [Getúlio Vargas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Get%C3%BAlio_Vargas) to serve in non-consecutive terms, and the first to unseat an incumbent president. He was sworn in on 1 January 2023.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257] At age 77, he was the oldest Brazilian president at the time of inauguration.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259][^cite_note-Boadle-260]

### Tenure

Lula said that his main commitments were: the reconstruction of the country in the face of the economic crisis; democracy, sovereignty and peace; economic development and stability; fighting poverty; education; implementation of a National System of Culture and the expansion of housing programmes.[^cite_note-261] He held the presidency of the [G20](2206608.md) from 2023 (succeeding [India](14533.md)) until the [2024 Brazilian G20 Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_G20_Rio_de_Janeiro_summit), where after this the presidency was handed over to [South Africa](17416221.md).[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263]

Lula's popularity declined; in September 2023, 38% of those polled considered him to be good or excellent, while 30% considered him to be average, and 31% viewed him as bad or terrible.[^cite_note-auto6-5]

On 23 October 2025, Lula announced that he would run for reelection as president in the [2026 Brazilian general election](72657051.md).[^cite_note-264]

#### Foreign policy

##### China



Lula and Chinese leader [Xi Jinping](2017814.md), April 2023

In March 2023, Lula met in [China](5405.md) with [Chinese leader](262766.md) [Xi Jinping](2017814.md), and signed 15 memoranda of understanding and 20 agreements on a wide range of issues.[^cite_note-265] Lula gave a speech in which he said that no one would keep Brazil from improving its relationship with China—which was taken as a reference to the United States.[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] In January 2024, Lula reaffirmed to Director of the [Office of the Central Foreign Affairs Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_the_Central_Foreign_Affairs_Commission) [Wang Yi](18211536.md) his recognition of China's policy of "[one China](66739.md)", under which China claims Taiwan.[^cite_note-268] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md), in the meantime, cautioned Lula against cozying up to China, while remaining silent about [China's human rights abuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_China).[^cite_note-auto11-105][^cite_note-269][^cite_note-auto13-270]

##### Cuba

In September 2023, Lula called Cuba a "victim" of an "illegal" [United States embargo against Cuba](1105954.md).[^cite_note-auto15-271][^cite_note-auto13-270] He also denounced the inclusion of Cuba on the [US list of state sponsors of terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Sponsors_of_Terrorism_\(U.S._list\)).[^cite_note-auto15-271][^cite_note-272]

##### Iran

In March 2023, Lula's administration allowed two [Iranian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Navy) warships, [forward base ship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expeditionary_Mobile_Base) [IRIS *Makran*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRIS_Makran) and [frigate](10946960.md) [IRIS *Dena*](74329424.md), to dock in Rio de Janeiro.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274][^cite_note-auto14-275] U.S. Ambassador to Brazil [Elizabeth Bagley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elizabeth_Bagley) said that in the past the warships had facilitated terrorist activities, and US Senator [Ted Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Cruz) said that "the docking of Iranian warships in Brazil is a dangerous development and a direct threat to the safety and security of Americans."[^cite_note-auto14-275]

Lula endorsed admitting Iran into the [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) organization, and in August 2023 met with Iranian president [Ebrahim Raeisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebrahim_Raeisi).[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-auto12-277] Lula stopped short of condemning [Iran's rights abuses](3156692.md).[^cite_note-auto12-277]

##### Nicaragua

In June 2023, at an [Organization of American States](22788.md) (OAS) summit, Lula tried to soften OAS criticism of Nicaragua's government, which was accused of repression and of violations of human rights and property rights.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280][^cite_note-auto16-281] Nicaragua's former ambassador to the OAS, [Arturo McFields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arturo_McFields), said that the proposed softening was "shameful," and that "President Lula is lying and telling another story that never existed in Nicaragua."[^cite_note-auto16-281]

##### Russia



Lula with Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) during the [Victory Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade) celebrations in Moscow on 9 May 2025

In May 2023, he declined an invitation to the [Saint Petersburg International Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Petersburg_International_Economic_Forum), saying that he "can't visit Russia at the moment", while confirming that he had spoken to Putin.[^cite_note-282] In December 2023, Lula said he would invite Vladimir Putin to the [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) and [G20 summits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_G20_Rio_de_Janeiro_summit) in Brazil. Due to Brazil being a signatory of the [Rome Statute](68762.md) of the [International Criminal Court](14880.md), Putin could be [placed under arrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_arrest_warrants_for_Russian_figures) by the Brazilian authorities if he sets foot on Brazil's territory. Lula said Putin could be arrested in Brazil, but that would be the decision of Brazil's [independent courts](26357061.md), not his government.[^cite_note-283] In February 2024, he was visited by Russian Foreign Minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md).[^cite_note-284] In May 2025, he attended the [Victory Day parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Moscow_Victory_Day_Parade) in Moscow.[^cite_note-285]

##### Russian invasion of Ukraine

Lula has commented often on the [Russo-Ukrainian War](42085878.md). He condemned the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine),[^cite_note-reu19-286] but Ukraine called some of his comments as "Russian attempts to distort the truth".[^cite_note-Reuters_ukraine_response-287][^cite_note-288]

In May 2022, Lula blamed both Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) and Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) for Russia's invasion of Ukraine, saying that Zelenskyy "is as responsible as Putin for the war".[^cite_note-289] Lula also repeatedly criticized [NATO](21133.md) and the [European Union](9317.md) for being partially responsible for the war. He accused NATO of "claiming for itself the right to install military bases in the vicinity of another country".[^cite_note-290] In April 2023, Lula declared after a state visit to China that "the United States needs to stop encouraging war and start talking about peace". [U.S. National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._National_Security_Council) spokesman [John Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kirby_\(admiral\)) responded by accusing Lula of "parroting Russian and Chinese propaganda", describing his comments as "simply misguided" and "suggesting the United States and Europe are somehow not interested in peace, or that we share responsibility for the war".[^cite_note-291]



Lula with Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md), 20 September 2023

In April 2023, Lula initially condemned Russia's violation of Ukraine's territorial integrity and said Russia should withdraw from Ukrainian territory it has occupied since February 2022.[^cite_note-reu19-286] Later, however, that same month, he suggested that Ukraine should "give up [Crimea](163045.md)" to Russia in exchange for [peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) and Russia's withdrawal from Ukrainian territory [it occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-occupied_territories_of_Ukraine) after February 2022, saying Zelenskyy "cannot want everything".[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293]

After Germany appealed to Lula to [provide military aid to Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_aid_to_Ukraine_during_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War) by selling it arms, Lula refused.[^cite_note-294] On 26 April, in a joint press conference, Spanish prime minister [Pedro Sánchez](43297512.md) questioned Lula's position, stressing that as the victimized country Ukraine needed to be supported.[^cite_note-elmu-295] Lula said that he is seeking peace in accordance with a binding foreign policy principle in the pacifist [Brazilian Constitution of 1988](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brazilian_Constitution_of_1988).[^cite_note-296] He said that the countries of the [Global South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_North_and_Global_South), including Brazil, India, Indonesia and China "want peace", but both Putin and Zelenskyy "are convinced that they are going to win the war" and do not want to talk about peace.[^cite_note-297] He noted the [human cost of the war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War), as well as the war's impact on [food security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022%E2%80%932023_food_crises), [energy costs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021%E2%80%932023_global_energy_crisis), and [global supply chains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_supply_chain).[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299]

##### Saudi Arabia



Lula with Saudi foreign minister [Faisal bin Farhan Al Saud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faisal_bin_Farhan_Al_Saud), 2023

In November 2023, Lula met in [Riyadh](51554.md) with the prime minister and crown prince of Saudi Arabia, [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-auto18-301][^cite_note-auto17-302] They discussed strengthening bilateral relations, and investments in both countries.[^cite_note-auto18-301][^cite_note-auto17-302] Salman said that a more robust strategic partnership between the two countries would benefit both sides.[^cite_note-auto18-301] The \$10 billion that the [sovereign wealth fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_wealth_fund) of Saudi Arabia pledged to invest in Brazil was one topic of conversation.[^cite_note-auto18-301][^cite_note-auto17-302] Lula mentioned Brazil's rapprochement with Arab countries.[^cite_note-auto18-301] Salman also discussed Saudi Arabia's entry into BRICS in January 2024.[^cite_note-auto18-301] Lula invited Salman to visit Brazil in 2024.[^cite_note-auto18-301][^cite_note-303][^cite_note-auto17-302]

In February 2024, Saudi Ambassador to Brazil Faisal Ghulam participated in a reception held by the ambassadors of [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_countries) and [Islamic countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_countries) in honour of Lula, and on behalf of the ambassadors of the Arab and Islamic countries, Ghulam delivered a speech in which he reviewed the developing relations between the Arab and Islamic countries and Brazil.[^cite_note-304]

##### Gaza war

Lula condemned the [Hamas attack on Israel](74998519.md) carried out on 7 October 2023.[^cite_note-305] On 11 October 2023, he called for a ceasefire in the Gaza war, stating, it was "urgently needed in defence of Israeli and Palestinian children".[^cite_note-306] Lula urged [Hamas](13913.md) to release [kidnapped](75025451.md) Israeli children and Israel to stop bombing the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) and allow Palestinian children and their mothers to leave the war zone.[^cite_note-307] On 25 October 2023, Lula stated, "It's not a war, it's a [genocide](75668427.md)".[^cite_note-308] On 18 February 2024, he drew comparisons to the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust), saying that the only historical equivalent was "when Hitler decided to kill the Jews."[^cite_note-auto-309] His remarks provoked outcry in Israel; he was accused of "blatant antisemitism" by [Dani Dayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dani_Dayan), the Chairman of [Yad Vashem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yad_Vashem).[^cite_note-310] Brazil's ambassador to Israel [Frederico Meyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederico_Meyer) was recalled after these comments, and President Lula was designated a *[persona non grata](521241.md)* in the State of Israel.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] Lula subsequently declined to apologise and despite having invoked a comparison with Adolf Hitler, he stated "I did not say the word Holocaust, that was the interpretation of the prime minister of Israel, it was not mine."[^cite_note-313]

##### United States



Lula and US President [Joe Biden](145422.md) at the [White House](33057.md) on 10 February 2023



Lula and US President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) in Kuala Lumpur on 26 October 2025

In April 2023, alluding to the support of the United States for Ukraine in the wake of the Russian invasion of Ukraine, Lula said that the US needs to "stop encouraging war."[^cite_note-314] In May 2023, he said that US economic sanctions on Venezuela were "worse than a war" and "kill" women and children.[^cite_note-315] In February 2024, [University of São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo) foreign policy expert Feliciano de Sa Guimaraes said Lula only listens to one side in his government, "the left-wing, anti-American voices who very aggressively speak of a radical change in the global order."[^cite_note-316]

Brazilian President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva has been making international headlines for standing his ground against Washington. After the G7 meeting, Lula reacted to President Trump's vocal support of the Bolsonaro family and how Brazil's judicial system dealt with them. "He is entitled to his electoral and ideological preferences. As far as I'm concerned, he can continue liking Bolsonaro, the father, the son, the grandson... there's no accounting for taste. Now, don't meddle in the Brazilian elections, because the Brazilian elections are a Brazilian problem, just as American elections are their business, not mine. All I want is the same respect for Brazil that I have for the United States. That's it."[^cite_note-317]

He also took direct aim at Trump's reported foreign policy remarks, suggesting the U.S. president does not understand Latin America. He was also critical of the president's desire to take control of Greenland, Panama and Canada. "The world is full of madmen," he said.[^cite_note-318]

##### Venezuela



Lula meeting with Venezuelan President [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md), May 2023

Lula restored diplomatic ties that Brazil had cut off with Venezuela's [Nicolas Maduro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Maduro) government after 2018 elections that had been [condemned by critics as a sham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis). In March 2023, Lula refused to join 54 other nations and sign a United Nations declaration criticizing Venezuela's human rights abuses.[^cite_note-319] In May 2023 Lula met with Maduro in Brazil.[^cite_note-320]

In May 2023, Lula warmly embraced and fully supported Venezuela's authoritarian leftist President Nicolás Maduro.[^cite_note-auto7-321] Lula dismissed charges against Maduro of [human rights](13831.md) and [civil rights](37071.md) abuses as a political "constructed narrative."[^cite_note-auto8-131] Lula was criticized by Uruguay's President [Luis Lacalle Pou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Lacalle_Pou), who said that the "worst thing we can do" is pretend there are no significant human rights problems in Venezuela, and by Chile's President [Gabriel Boric](47329497.md), who said that Lula was making light of [human rights violations in Venezuela](23438021.md).[^cite_note-auto7-321] Lula also criticized as "unjustified" [U.S. sanctions on Venezuela](60408367.md) for its alleged human rights abuses, and criticized the United States for denying the legitimacy of Maduro, who the U.S. said had not allowed free elections.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324]

In August 2024, amid [Venezuelan protests](77454821.md) against Maduro, Lula described Maduro's government as "a very unpleasant regime" with an "authoritarian slant", but not a dictatorship.[^cite_note-\:5-325] Regarding Maduro's victory in the [2024 Venezuelan presidential election](60825396.md), which UN election experts said lacked "basic transparency and integrity", Lula said that Maduro should either hold new elections or form a [coalition government](6036.md). Both Maduro and the opposition rejected these suggestions.[^cite_note-\:5-325]

#### Economy

Further information: [Bolsa Família](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsa_Fam%C3%ADlia)



Lula and other BRICS leaders at the [15th BRICS Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/15th_BRICS_summit), August 2023, in Johannesburg

In March 2023, Lula reinforced the *Bolsa Família* program. The programme was created during the first term of Lula and then significantly cut by [Jair Bolsonaro](46603496.md), with its goal being help to around 60 million Brazilians suffering from poverty.[^cite_note-326] According to [the World Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_Bank) estimates, the reinforced programme would reduce the poverty rate in Brazil to 24.3% – the level before the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md).[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

In August 2023, Lula announced a vast infrastructure investment programme of over \$350 billion over four years. Part of this sum is earmarked to finance the "My home, my life" social housing project. It also includes 100 billion for energy and 65 billion for transport and roads. Education and health are also concerned, with the construction of schools and hospitals. The project also aims to boost economic growth and develop [clean energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clean_energy).[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330]

At the beginning of September 2023, he presented a major plan to eradicate hunger, as 33 million Brazilians do not have enough to eat, and more than half the country is affected to varying degrees by some form of food insecurity. To this end, he set up a national network of food banks to prevent waste, increased the budget allocated to school meals, and increased the purchase of food from family farms to supply public canteens. These measures are part of a broader policy to build social housing and raise the minimum wage and other social benefits. The fight against world hunger is also high on the Brazilian president's international agenda.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332]

In the first quarter of 2023, Brazilian economy grew by 1.9%. In the second quarter, by 0.9%, 3 times more than expected, while many of the neighbors of Brazil saw a shrinkage in their economy. The possible reasons of this phenomenon included reduced inflation, a good harvest, and an improved credit rating. The economic policy of Lula regarding taxation, spending, public ownership of some companies probably played a major role in this. Explaining his economic philosophy Lula once said: ""\[Brazilians] need to understand that the money that exists in this country needs to circulate in the hands of many people," "We do not want the concentration of wealth. We want more people to have access to credit to make the wheel of the economy turn. The growing economy needs to be distributed."[^cite_note-333]

In 2025, Lula became the first Brazilian president since 1992 to have one of his decrees overturned by [Congress](690287.md) following its rejection of a proposal to raise a financial transactions tax.[^cite_note-334]

#### Environment



Launch of the [Global Biofuel Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Biofuel_Alliance) at the [2023 G20 New Delhi summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_G20_New_Delhi_summit)

During his campaign, Lula pledged to end [illegal logging](465780.md).[^cite_note-335] In 2004, Lula had presented a road map for curbing [deforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation). It was part of "The Action Plan for the Prevention and Control of Deforestation in the Legal Amazon", which sought to decrease deforestation in the Amazon by 80% by 2020.[^cite_note-336] This plan was largely responsible for the 83% decrease in the Amazon deforestation rate in the years 2004 through 2012, but it was suspended during Bolsonaro's presidency. Lula re-affirmed the plan's goals in his third term, with a new target of zero illegal deforestation by the year 2030.[^cite_note-337] The plan includes different measures for creating a sustainable economy in the Amazon region, like [bioeconomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bioeconomy), rural credits and managed fishing.[^cite_note-338]



The [Amazon rainforest](48139.md) near [Manaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manaus), Brazil

According to Amazon Conservation's MAAP forest monitoring programme, the deforestation rate in the Brazilian Amazon from the 1 January to the 8 November 2023 decreased by 59% in comparison to the same period in 2022.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340][^cite_note-341] In July 2023 the deforestation rate was 66% lower than in July 2022. In the beginning of August Lula participated in the Belem summit, 8 Amazonian nations renewed [the Amazon cooperation treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_Cooperation_Treaty_Organization).[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343] However, there are concerns that illegal loggers have partly moved their action from the Amazon rainforest to [Cerrado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerrado), where the environmental destruction has increased.[^cite_note-344] As a whole, the rate of primary forest loss declined in Brazil by 36% in 2023.[^cite_note-345]

Bolsonaro had strongly cut spending for security in the Brazilian Amazon, and in 2022, 34 environmental defenders were murdered in this region. When Lula re-assumed office, he sent troops to restore law enforcement in the region. In October 2023, there were still "reports of violence, threats, torture, intimidation, attempts at criminalization and other non-lethal violations".[^cite_note-346]

In April, Biden pledged to give \$500 million to the [Amazon Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Amazon_Fund) which was frozen during the rule of Bolsonaro and reactivated when Lula returned to power, to deal with [climate change](5042951.md)".[^cite_note-347] According to [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry), the overall financial help from US to Brazil for stopping deforestation through different channels will be around 2 billion US dollars.[^cite_note-348]

Lula and French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) agreed about cooperation between Brazil and France on different environmental issues, including the transmission of 1.1 billion dollars for preserving the Amazon rainforest.[^cite_note-349]



Lula visits the [Yanomami people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yanomami) in the Brazilian state of [Roraima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roraima) during the [Yanomami humanitarian crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yanomami_humanitarian_crisis) in January 2023

Lula pledged to recognize 14 new [indigenous reserves](31287705.md). Six were recognized as of May 2023.[^cite_note-350] Lula and American president Joe Biden committed to work together on the issue.[^cite_note-351]

Several hours after Lula talked about leaving fossil fuels at the [2023 United Nations Climate Change Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_United_Nations_Climate_Change_Conference) (also known as COP28), his government held an auction in which it offered 603 territorial blocks for oil extraction. The territories cover 2% of the territory of Brazil, overlap with many protected areas or areas belonging to indigenous people and can result in a release of 1 gigaton of CO<sub>2</sub>.[^cite_note-352]

Lula has expressed support for the paving of [BR-319](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BR-319_\(Brazil_highway\)), a project initiated by the Bolsonaro government. Although he argues that the project can be done sustainably, one study found that the road could enable [deforestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deforestation) on a scale of territory the size of [Florida](18933066.md) by 2030. A court blocked the project in July 2024, saying that the government lacked a plan to combat the deforestation that would follow the implementation of the project.[^cite_note-353]

In November 2025, Lula took the unusual step for a head of state of personally attending the [COP30](75227876.md) conference in [Belem](409573.md) in an attempt to smooth over geopolitical rifts preventing a climate deal from being reached.[^cite_note-354]

#### Freedom of the press

In March 2023, the Lula government launched a campaign to fight "misinformation".[^cite_note-auto25-355][^cite_note-356] The initiative was viewed by many as a tool for Lula's administration to delegitimize criticism it faces—under the guise of "fact-checking", and raised serious concerns about freedom of expression.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-auto25-355] In response, the senior programme director of the [International Centre for Journalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Center_for_Journalists), Christina Tardáliga, tweeted "There is no such thing as government fact-checking. This appropriation of the term is misguided and offensive. What the government does is propaganda."[^cite_note-auto25-355]

#### Health problems

In late October 2024, Lula suffered a fall in the official residence, which resulted in trauma to the back of his head and a small brain hemorrhage in the temporal-frontal region, prompting him to cancel, under advice from his doctors, a planned trip to a [BRICS summit in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16th_BRICS_summit).[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] In December of the same year, he was admitted to hospital after complaining of a headache. A brain haemorrhage was discovered after an [MRI scan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MRI_scan), and an emergency [craniotomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craniotomy) was performed. The [intracranial haemorrhage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intracranial_haemorrhage) was attributed to his fall in October. After the operation he was reported to be recovering in [intensive care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intensive_care).[^cite_note-360]

In May 2025, Lula was diagnosed with [labyrinthitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labyrinthitis) after suffering from vertigo.[^cite_note-361] In January 2026, Lula underwent cataract surgery on his left eye at a hospital in Brasilia.[^cite_note-362]

#### [2026 Brazilian general election](72657051.md)

In September 2026, President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva campaigned for a fourth, non-consecutive presidential term. His campaign emphasized social and economic policies, while his administration also promoted measures to reduce domestic fuel prices amid higher international oil prices. Lula remained one of the two leading candidates ahead of the first-round vote scheduled for 4 October.[^cite_note-363]

Lula has advocated "[socialism of the 21st century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism_of_the_21st_century)", but Lulism is considered to be substantially similar to [social liberalism](765430.md).[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-SocialLiberal-365][^cite_note-366] Although he showed a moderate centre-left liberal tendency economically, he highlighted his closeness with the [Bolivarian Republic of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivarian_Republic_of_Venezuela) and negatively evaluated [Juan Guaidó](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Guaid%C3%B3) during the [Venezuelan crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_crisis).[^cite_note-367] He is "personally against" abortion, but maintains that it should be treated as a [public health issue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unsafe_abortion#Health_risks).[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-369]

### Palestine

Lula criticized the decisions by [Western countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_countries) to cut funding to [UNRWA](32270.md) and in response pledged to the [Palestinian government](241405.md) that Brazil would increase its funding to UNRWA. Lula has called for a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution) with Palestine "definitively recognised as a full and sovereign state".[^cite_note-auto-309]

On 18 February 2024, Lula told reporters in [Addis Ababa, Ethiopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Addis_Ababa,_Ethiopia), while attending the African Union Summit, "What's happening in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) isn't a war, it's a [genocide](12441.md). ... It's not a war of soldiers against soldiers. It's a war between a highly prepared army and women and children. ... What's happening in the Gaza Strip with the [Palestinian](23267.md) people hasn't happened at any other moment in history. Actually, it has happened: when [Hitler](2731583.md) decided to kill the [Jews](25955086.md)".[^cite_note-auto-309]

  [^cite_note-370]

## In popular culture

Academy Award-nominated Brazilian director [Fábio Barreto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A1bio_Barreto) directed the 2009 Brazilian biographical film *[Lula, Son of Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lula,_Son_of_Brazil)* that depicts the life of Lula up to 35 years of age.[^cite_note-417] Upon its release, it was the most expensive Brazilian film ever.[^cite_note-418] The film was a commercial and critical failure.[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420] Critics charged that it was election propaganda, fostering a [cult of personality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cult_of_personality).[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423]

The Netflix Brazilian series *[The Mechanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Mechanism_\(TV_series\))* deals with Operation Car Wash, and has a character inspired by Lula, who is referred to as João Higino in the series.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425]

The 2019 Brazilian documentary, *[The Edge of Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Edge_of_Democracy)*, written and directed by [Petra Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petra_Costa), chronicled the rise and fall of Lula and Dilma Rousseff, and the socio-political upheaval in Brazil during the period.[^cite_note-426] Lula is also featured in the director's 2024 documentary *[Apocalypse in the Tropics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apocalypse_in_the_Tropics)*.[^cite_note-427]
